## Document 001: 10 月の金融政策決定会合は利上げ見送りへ 政策金利の２％超えは？

Source PDF: all report pdf/10 月の金融政策決定会合は利上げ見送りへ 政策金利の２％超えは？.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-14
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

2026 年 10 月 14 日(水)

1/3 
10 月の金融政策決定会合は利上げ見送りへ  
政策金利の２％超えは？ 
 経済調査部主席エコノミスト藤代宏一 
（℡：050-5474-6123）

・10月30日の金融政策決定会合に先立って、早くも観測記事が金融市場を賑わせている。米系
通信は「日銀は今月会合で利上げ見送る公算大、12月の可能性排除せず」と報じた。記事内
では「経済・物価は日銀の見通しに沿って推移しており、２会合連続での利上げが必要なほ
ど物価の上振れリスクが一段と強まっている情勢にはない」、とはいえ「一時的な要因を除
いた基調的な物価上昇率は上昇を続けており、目標の２％から上振れていくリスクには依然
として注意が必要だ」などとする日銀関係者の発言が記述されていた。また既報のとおり、
基調的物価上昇率が概ね２％に達したとの判断を展望レポートに記すことにも触れられてい
た。

・10月の利上げ見送りの根拠として、昨日発表された９月の企業物価指数は一定の存在感があ
る。国内企業物価指数は前月比▲0.3％、前年比＋6.8％と上昇が一服し、市場予想の同＋
7.6％も下回った。原油価格が高止まりするなか、世界的なＡＩ・半導体関連需要の拡大など
を背景に非鉄金属や化学製品などで上昇が続いた一方、米を中心とする農林水産物の価格下
落が下押しに寄与した。円ベースの輸入物価指数は前年比＋24.5％と大幅な上昇を記録した
・日経平均株価は先行き12 ヶ月72,000 円程度で推移するだろう 
・USD/JPY は先行き12 ヶ月 155 円程度で推移するだろう 
・日銀は政策金利を12 月に 1.5％とした後、27 年末までに2.25％とするだろう 
・FED はFF 金利を、年内は4.0％で据え置くだろう

### Page 2

2/3 
が、８月の同＋25.1％からは小幅に鈍化した。川上段階のインフレ圧力は依然として強いも
のの、９月会合時点の判断に大きな修正を迫るものではなかったと考えられる。

・同日発表された「生活意識に関するアンケート調査」（９月調査）も似たような結果であっ
た。５年後の物価予想については「かなり上がる」の割合が51.1％と前回調査対比で概ね横
ばいとなり、「少し上がる」との合計では86.5％と高止まりした。他方、今後５年間の物価
上昇率（年平均）は、中央値が５％で横ばいも、平均値は＋10.1％と前回調査対比で0.7％pt
低下した。ガソリン代の補助に加え、米価の下落が効いたとみられ、家計の予想物価上昇率
が一段と上放れする事態は回避された。政府による家計支援策が効いているとはいえ、家計
の予想物価上昇率が低下したことは、日銀に時間的余裕を与え得る。なお、景況感について
は「１年前との比較」と「１年後の予想」の双方とも大きな変化は認められなかった。

### Page 3

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
3/3 
・先行きの金融政策はどうなるであろうか。筆者は、10月会合は利上げ見送り、12月会合で利
上げを決定し、政策金利は1.5％で越年すると予想する。Ｆｅｄの利上げ観測が燻り、円安圧
力が強いなか、日銀が金融引き締め方向への手を緩めるとなれば、一段と円安が進行しかね
ない。また日銀短観（９月調査）を見る限り、企業の金融環境に大きな変化はみられず、既
往の利上げが実体経済に染み渡っている様子はそれほど窺えない。これらを踏まえ、筆者は
2027年も３～４回の利上げがあり、政策金利は２％を超えることを想定している。

・ただし、Ｆｅｄが2027年に政策金利を据え置き、その次の政策変更が利下げになるとの見方
が生じれば、日銀は円安対策から解放され、利上げを中断ないしは終了する可能性がある。
その点、ウィリアムズ・ＮＹ連銀総裁とウォラー理事の発言は注視しておきたい。まずウィ
リアムズ総裁は10月13日に「来年はインフレがかなり鈍化し、通年でみれば、全体のインフ
レ率は目標⁠の２％近辺になるとみている。これは私が言及した要因、すなわち関税の影響が
完全に過去のものとなることを反映している」として、2027年に利上げは必要ないとの見方
を示した。

・⁠ウォラー理事は10月13日に「最近の米国債利回りの上昇は経済成長の加速とＡＩへの大規模
投資によるもので、インフレ期待の高まりが原因ではない」、「インフレ期待は引き続き
『しっかりと安定している』」と述べ、その上で「データセンター建設を含むＡＩ投資が経
済の需要を支えており、今後２～３年はその効果が続く⁠可能性がある。大幅な生産性向上が
実現するのは、なお『数年先』だ」とした。同理事の発言を整理すると、①予想インフレは
安定しており、その点において現在の金融政策は適切である、②ＡＩ投資が一巡すれば、そ
れに起因するインフレ圧力は鈍化する、③その後に生産性向上によるディスインフレ方向へ
の動きが予想される、といったところであろう。

・両氏の予想が現実のものになれば、Ｆｅｄの利上げ観測は後退し、場合によっては2027年に
利下げ観測が台頭することもあり得る。そうした環境下、日銀は利上げペースを緩める、或
いは終了を検討するなど、多くの選択肢を手にすることができる。

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 聯準會上周三決議升息一碼至 3.75%-4.00%區間，FOMC 政策聲明顯示，18 名
FOMC 成員中有 12 人預計今年將再升息一次 ， 另有4 人認為存在升息兩次的可
能性 ，加上聯準會主席華許(Kevin Warsh)會後表達控制通膨的偏鷹立場 ， 帶動美
元指數走升至 100 高位。
⚫ 歐元區 9 月 ZEW 經濟景氣指數自前月的 31.4 降至 25.8，低於預估的 39.9。數
據下滑顯示在美伊衝突未獲緩解 、 能源價格持續高位情況下 ， 市場對於歐元區經
濟前景抱持較不樂觀的態度。
⚫ 美國公布初請失業金人數為 19.6 萬人，優於市場預期的 20.8 萬，再度呼應聯準
會主席華許(Kevin Warsh)認為目前勞動力市場仍具韌性的說法， 在美國經濟穩
健及通膨頑固的情況下，預計聯準會將持續升息，有利美元持穩。
⚫ 歐洲央行管委暨葡萄牙央行總裁佩雷拉(Alvaro Santos Pereira)表示，歐元區目前

通膨雖未廣泛擴散 ， 惟考量近期天然氣價格飆升 ， 央行將密切關注未來通膨壓力
擴散情形。最新利率期貨市場顯示，歐洲央行今年底仍有再升息一碼空間。
⚫ 日本政府通過消費稅減稅及家庭現金補貼的政策大綱，預計自明年 4 月起的兩
年內，將食品消費稅率自 8%降至 1%，惟大綱中未明確說明資金來源，再度引
發市場對於日本政府的財政擔憂，短線不利日圓表現。
⚫ 台灣央行 9/17 宣布重貼現率連 10 凍維持在 2%不變，央行總裁楊金龍表示，台
灣的通膨相較其他主要經濟體溫和，採取漸進緊縮貨幣政策有助物價穩定。 此
外，目前通膨率為 2%，明年有望降至 2%以下，後續將觀察通膨發展、主要經
濟體貨幣政策緊縮程度及台灣金融情勢，以決定未來貨幣政策走向。

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Sep/21/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週台幣匯價高點在 31.650，低點在 31.958，以 31.808 作收，較前一周收盤價
升值 1 角 7 分。 
上週台幣震盪走貶 ， 美國CPI 數據公布後加強市場對於聯準會 9 月升息的預期 ，
早盤台幣跳空開低，外資連續三日賣超台股並匯出，加以出口商態度轉為惜售，令
台幣接連貶破 31.700、31.800、31.900 關卡， 週四由於聯準會偏鷹派升息，美元指
數走升至 100.300，早盤台幣跳空開低，台幣最低貶至31.958，不過隨後出口商賣壓
出籠，外資雙向進出，台幣收復部分跌幅，維持在31.900 附近震盪；臨近周末，外
資買超台股並於外匯市場匯入 ， 台幣走升至31.722， 最終在央行調節之下收在31.808。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週台幣區間震盪 。 就國際市場而言 ， 美國最新初領失業救濟金人數較預
期減少一萬多人、續領失業救濟金人數亦下降，顯示美國勞動力市場尚屬穩健，加
以聯準會升息抗通膨，鷹派立場給予美元指數支撐、不利台幣表現；就台灣市場來
看，九月台灣央行並未升息，台美利差擴大預計將帶給台幣壓力，此外，儘管聯準
會升息利空出盡，外資買超台股帶動台幣走升，惟台灣即將迎來中秋連假，預計金
融市場交投謹慎，將令台幣陷入橫盤整理格局，綜合以上，預估本週台幣走勢區間
震盪。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.600-32.000

### Page 4

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Sep/21/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣震盪走升。人民幣中間價連續走升，接連升破 6.7700、6.7600 整數
關口，升至三年七個月來高點，帶動人民幣偏升，不過由於美元指數震盪走強，加
以市場靜待聯準會利率決議公布 ， 交投謹慎 ， 人民幣升幅有限 ， 大致維持高檔震盪 ，
週四聯準會鷹派升息一碼，惟人民幣中間價連續五日走升至 6.7521，並創下四年高
點，帶動人民幣緩步走升，USD/CNH 升破 6.7000 整數大關，終場境外人民幣收在
6.6944，週線升值 94 點，境內人民幣則收在 6.6984，週線升值 21 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣震盪走升 。 就國際市場來看 ，FED 最新政策聲明和經濟預測顯
示，聯準會為明年繼續收緊貨幣政策敞開了大門，美元指數走升，抑制人民幣升值
的速度；就中國國內市場來看，中間價持續偏升將帶動人民幣走升，加以出口商實
質拋匯需求將支撐人民幣表現； 最後就技術面來看 ，USD/CNH 日線圖中 ， 匯價維持
在五日均線之下交投，均線呈現空頭排列，加以USD/CNH 有效跌破 6.7000 整數關
卡，顯示 USD/CNH 空方勢力較盛，綜合以上，預估本週人民幣震盪走升。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6700-6.7200

### Page 5

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Sep/21/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週日圓走貶。 週初，美國通膨數據維持高位 ，令聯準會升息預期升溫 ，USD/JPY
自 153.29 低點反彈至 155 附近；週間 ，美國聯準會宣布升息一碼 ， 帶動USD/JPY 上
漲至 156.41；臨近週末，日本央行如市場預期升息一碼，惟央行偏鴿派立場令日圓
續貶，帶動 USD/JPY 一度最高來到 158.05，而隨後在日本政府於外匯市場進行匯率
詢價下，USD/JPY 回吐部分漲幅，終場收在 156.86，週線上漲 349 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、日本政府上周通過消費稅減
稅及家庭現金補貼政策大綱，惟大綱中未明確說明財源，再度引發市場對於日本政
府財政惡化的擔憂，預計將不利日圓走勢。二、日本央行上周五決議將政策利率調
升一碼至 1.25%， 9 名決策委員中有 2 人對升息持反對意見，降低市場對日本央行
進一步升息的預期。另一方面 ， 聯準會上周三亦升息一碼至3.75%-4.00%區間 ，FOMC
政策聲明顯示，18 位官員中有 12 人預計今年將再升息一次，另有 4 人認為存在升
息兩次的可能性，聯準會升息立場明顯較日本央行更為鷹派，預計將支撐USD/JPY
走勢。三、日本財務大臣片山皋月近期再度重申，將於必要時採取外匯干預行動，
以維護外匯市場有序運行。四、就技術面而言，在日線圖中，USD/JPY 短中天期均
線向下發散走勢偏空，惟近期匯價在多次向下測試 154 均拉回，明顯下方有買盤支
撐。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：154.80-159.50

美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
             Sep/21/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 下跌。週初，聯準會升息預期升溫加上歐元區 9 月經濟景氣指
數下滑，令歐元走跌至 1.1525；週間，美國聯準會鷹派升息立場帶動美元走升，歐
元最低來到 1.1460；臨近週末，歐元持續偏弱整理，終場收在 1.1483，週線下跌 107
點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 震盪偏弱，主要原因如下：一、 歐洲央行管委卡薩克斯
(Martins Kazaks) 表示 ， 為避免美伊戰爭引發的燃料成本上漲開始傳導至工資和其他
商品價格，歐洲央行可能需要漸進式調升利率以抑制通膨。最新利率期貨市場顯示
歐洲央行今年仍有升息一碼的空間，預計將支撐歐元走勢。二、美國聯準會決議將
利率調升一碼至 3.75%-4.00%區間，聯準會主席華許會後偏鷹言論帶動美元指數走
升至 100 上方，預計將限縮歐元走升空間。三、歐元區 9 月 ZEW 經濟景氣指數自
前月的 31.4 降至 25.8，低於預估的 39.9。數據下滑顯示在美伊衝突未獲緩解、能源
價格持續高位情況下，市場對於歐元區經濟前景抱持較不樂觀的態度，將不利歐元
表現。四、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD 近期回測年線未能有效突破後明顯
拉回轉為下行走勢，目前匯價承壓於各天期均線下方，空方動能較佔優勢。綜合以
上，預估 EUR/USD 本週呈現震盪偏弱的可能性較高。

＜預估本週＞ 歐元兌美元：1.1350-1.1590

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分 ， 本週央行理監事會議決議結果 ， 維持政策利率不變 ， 符合
市場預期 ， 且近兩週央行持續減發定存單 ， 令市場短錢充裕 ， 惟週五銀行間分
配略為不均，部分銀行有資金需求，因此短率持穩於近期區間。 
週 五 隔 夜 拆 款 利 率 區 間 在 0.805%~0.830% ， 30 天 期 短 票 主 要 成 交 在
1.67%~1.70%，利率高位較上週上升 1 個基本點。 
二、 本週市場交投清淡，5 年期指標券 A15108 週三等殖系統成交在 1.86%，10 年
期指標券 A15106 則未成交，最近成交利率為 1.92~1.921%。 
台灣央行理監事會議如市場預期維持政策利率不變 ， 總裁楊金龍表示 ， 今年國
內通膨仍屬可控，明年有望回落至 2%以下，市場對今年升息疑慮有所降溫，
對台債形成支撐 ， 惟國際債市走勢仍偏空 ， 預期台債殖利率短線仍呈高檔區間
盤整格局，後續關注下週三財政部公布的第四季公債發行計劃，預估 5 年券
區間為 1.79%~1.83%，10 年券區間為 1.90%~1.95%。

Key Takeaways from Company Meetings

Key Takeaways from Applied Materials Meeting 
Participants: Kevin Moraes, VP , Strategy and Marketing; Sree Kesapragada, VP , 
Advanced Packaging; Mike Sullivan, Head of Investor Relations 
Investor questions mainly focused on clarifications tied to the company’s investor event 
and financial targets. On revenue and capital intensity, management noted that 
semiconductor industry pricing is a key variable that it cannot control - but has high 
confidence in its framework tied to increasing WFE intensity per node - and these 
increases have already been programmatically locked in by customers today. On 
Applied’s target of 200bps of market share gain, management noted the complex set of 
mix effects involved in the calculation, but noted the substantial impact from China 
export control exclusions. Relative to the company’s newly articulated gross margin 
target of 54% by 2030, Applied believes there is the potential for upside to this target - 
but pointed out that the vast majority of its costs are variable in nature. On its optical 
tool portfolio, Applied believes that it can leverage its expertise and market position in 
e-beam technology to expand into this adjacent $9bn market. On AGS, management has 
high confidence in its outlook for mid-teens long term revenue growth - but noted that 
its services attach rate is higher on its newer tools relative to its installed fleet. 
Key Takeaways from Lam Research Meeting 
Participants: Ram Ganesh - VP of Investor Relations 
Consistent with prior commentary, Lam expects 8-10 new fab projects to come online in 
2027 - and currently expects product growth to be driven by DRAM, followed by 
advanced foundry/logic, followed by NAND flash. The company has not specified a 
timeframe for achieving its new long-term gross margin target of ~55%. However, we 
expect this to be achieved over several years, and see it tied more to product mix 
(particularly with new products such as Akara) and overseas operational manufacturing 
efficiencies rather than like-for-like pricing. Management remains constructive on the 
company’s competitive positioning in Logic, DRAM, and advanced packaging - 
particularly as etch and deposition intensity continues to grow with a variety of new 
device structures coming into the mix - including hard masks, surface prep, TSVs, and 
other materials engineering steps. 
Key Takeaways from Teradyne Meeting 
Participants: Roy Chorev - VP & GM of Compute Test Division; Amy McAndrews - VP of 
Investor Relations 
Teradyne announced a strategic partnership with and investment in FormFactor at 
SEMICON West. Teradyne remains committed to an open ecosystem of probe card 
vendors, but believes Form’s strong position in silicon photonics warrants a closer 
relationship that enables Teradyne to commit additional engineering platform resources. 
Regarding its XPU business, Teradyne continues to expect to gain merchant GPU share in 
the long run - and noted that Rubin test times are nearly 2X Blackwell given the near 
doubling in transistor count. Teradyne is also pursuing the tester opportunity at Intel, 
which is focused on increasing its exposure to external vendors (currently using an

internal solution) beginning with its foundry business, where Teradyne sees a
medium-term opportunity. In its memory business, Teradyne expects minimal impact
from customer de-specing of HBM as its demand is tied primarily to unit volume rather
than stack height. In co-packaged optics, Teradyne continues to see a very strong
product ramp opportunity, although given the early stages of development, there are
likely to be significant shifts between testing intensity at different insertions during the

manufacturing process.
Key Takeaways from Camtek Investor Meeting
Participants: Lior Aviram - Executive Chairman; Rafi Amit - CEO; Ramy Langer -
President & COO; Moshe Eisenberg - CFO; Eyal Segev - Marketing Director
We attended Camtek’s Investor Meeting at SEMICON. Our key takeaways are as follows:
Long-term financial targets: In conjunction with its investor meeting, Camtek n
pre-announced 3Q26 revenue of $160 mn –$161 mn (above its prior guidance),

guided to ~$180 mn for 4Q26, and provided an initial 2027 revenue outlook of $800
mn based on orders received to date. Management laid out a long-term revenue
target of $1.2 billion in about three years, with a gross margin target of 55%-56%
and operating margin target of 34%-36%.
Advanced packaging remains the key growth driver: Camtek stressed that n
advanced packaging remains its core business, accounting for ~80% of recent
orders, and expects advanced packaging wafer growth to run at about twice the

broader industry rate through 2031 (13% vs. 7% CAGR). Within advanced packaging,
the company expects the high-end of the market (which accounts for the vast
majority of its business) to grow at a ~24% CAGR through 2031, driven by AI, silicon
photonics, denser interconnects, and increasingly complex inspection and metrology
requirements.
Technology and product roadmap centered on inspection and specialized n
metrology: Camtek’s roadmap remains centered on its margin-accretive Hawk and

Eagle products for inspection (~85% of its 2026 mix), as well as its metrology
platforms. Separately, management also noted it is embedding AI into detection and
classification and plans to launch AI-enabled software in 2027 (which it attributed to
its Visual Layer acquisition).
M&A strategy focused on capability acquisition and strategic expansion: Camtek n
noted that it is pursuing both smaller acquisitions that add technology and/or talent
to its core business, as well as larger strategic transactions that broaden its offering,

while emphasizing that its $1.2 bn revenue target and financial model do not assume
meaningful M&A activity.
Key Takeaways from Onto Innovation Meeting
Participants: Brian Roberts - CFO; Sidney Ho - VP of Investor Relations
Onto remains very constructive on the demand outlook through 2H26 and heading into
2027 across a range of demand drivers - with growth likely to accelerate next year given
the growth of incremental market opportunities. In HBM, Onto believes its business is

likely to be minimally impacted by any “de-spec” activity by XPU vendors, as its tool
14 October 2026   3
Goldman Sachs Americas Technology: SemiconductorsFor the exclusive use of KEVINLU@LENOVO.COM
e92c7a75ab8b4efbba794e6b187208c8

### Page 4

demand is far more driven by unit volume than stack height. The company continues to
see CoWoS as a key demand driver that is increasingly driven by OSAT partners rather
than TSMC; Onto sees EMIB-T as a significant opportunity that promises to be material in
2028. From a competitive standpoint, Onto remains highly confident in its market share
position, including at TSMC - and sees no imminent impact from potential product
launches by competitors. On margins, management remains confident in its target of

350bps of gross margin expansion, which it believes can be achieved solely by product
mix and efficiencies in its manufacturing operations. The company believes it has room
to grow revenue substantially from projected levels, and does not see itself as capacity
constrained.
Key Takeaways from MKS Instruments Meeting
Participants: John Lee - President & CEO; Paretosh Misra - VP of Investor Relations
MKS believes the industry is in the early-to-middle stages of a substantial cyclical

industry upturn driven by AI and datacenter demand. The company is currently
preparing for a potential WFE spending envelope of up to $300bn in 2028, and
potentially higher over time. In the near-term, management expects the company to
potentially outgrow its customers (2Q26 was the first time the company delivered Y/Y
Semiconductor revenue growth in excess of them) given supply chain inventory effects.
MKS believes it has enough capacity to support WFE of ~$250bn with its Penang facility

(with a surge capacity of another 20%), but may need to expand capacity further if
these forecasts come to fruition. Importantly, the company does not believe it is
constraining its customers’ growth plans today. MKS has not taken significant pricing
actions, but could do so selectively in order to capture the value it delivers. Management
believes its product portfolio is well positioned, but would consider a possible
divestiture of its specialty industrial product lines at an attractive valuation.

### Page 5

Valuation/Risks

AMAT (Buy, on CL): Our 12-month price target of $670 is based on a 32X P/E multiple 
applied to our normalized EPS estimate of $21.00. Key downside risks include: (1) 
incremental export restrictions; (2) local China-based supplier share gains. 
LRCX (Buy): Our price target is $380, which is based on a 35X multiple applied to our 
normalized EPS forecast of $10.75. Key downside risks: (1) softer DRAM/HBM demand; 
(2) delays in planned clean room and fab expansions; (3) lower-than-expected content 
gains from advanced packaging and 3D architectures. 
TER (Buy): Our 12-month price target is $465, which is based on a 37X P/E multiple 
applied to our normalized EPS forecast of $12.50. Key downside risks: (1) delays in the 
revenue ramp with a merchant GPU customer, (2) significant quarterly volatility due to 
the lumpy nature of large AI-related infrastructure projects, (3) slower-than-expected 
recovery in Mobile. 
ONTO (Buy): Our12-month price target is $400, which is based on 32X our normalized 
EPS estimate of $12.50. Our target P/E is in line with peer multiples, and it is on the 
higher end of ONTO’s historical median, given recent momentum around HPC. Key 
upside/downside risks: (1) additional volume purchase agreements and accelerated 
customer adoption of Dragonfly G5; (2) shift towards OSATs or domestic Chinese 
manufacturers; (3) competitor launching a rival tool. 
CAMT (Neutral): Our 12-m price target of $160 is based on a 38X P/E multiple applied 
to our normalized EPS of $4.20. Key upside/downside risks: (1) notable push-outs in 
chiplet architectures; (2) failure to pass on higher pricing; (3) better-than-expected 
growth in HPC-driven revenue in the medium-term. 
MKSI (Sell): Our 12-month price target is $270, which is based on a 20X P/E multiple 
applied to our normalized EPS of $13.50. Key upside risks: (1) rebound in the NAND 
market from an equipment ordering perspective; (2) greater realization of synergies 
from the Atotech acquisition; (3) any material pickup in momentum for the company’s 
Semiconductor business due to broader WFE trends.

6274 台燿

報告日期： 2026/10/08 研 究 員：方姿婷  (Lesley Fang) 
壹、投 資 建 議 
重點摘要： 
 2Q26 營收符合預期， 毛利率受 1H26 漲價提升 ， 預期 3Q26 毛利
將順利反應成本上漲與優化之產品組合 ， 毛利率預估提升 3%+ 。  
 AI 伺服器帶動高階銅箔基板需求強勁，同時驅動高階網路交換
器升級動能，銅箔基板需要更高速傳輸、訊號低損耗之下會陸
續採用 CCL M8、M9 規格。 
 2H26 低軌 S 客戶 V3 版本新型火箭搭載低軌衛星，所採用之銅
箔基板升級至 M8 等級；以及，1.6T 光模組需求量增帶動銅箔
基板需求量增。

一. 2Q26 營收與財報符合市場預期，3Q26 營收優於預期  
台燿 2Q26 營收為 143.01 億元，+42.2%QoQ、+110.93%YoY，毛利
率為 29.72% ，稅後淨利為 23.42 億元，單季稅後 EPS+7.82 元。2Q26
台燿受到 ASIC CCL 專案出貨帶動，產品組合優化，毛利率已相較
1Q26 季增 4.5% 。台燿已公告 3Q26 營收為 179.48 億元(優於市場預
期 3%)，+25.50%QoQ，毛利率可順利轉嫁且高階 CCL 出貨比重提
升，預估台燿 3Q26 毛利率為 33.21%，稅後淨利 36.72 億元，單季
稅後 EPS+12.60 元。

二. AI 伺服器帶動 CCL/PCB 產值提升，產能擴充 33% 
宏遠投顧觀察，AI 伺服器與先進封裝等需求日增，高階 CCL 市場
需求將在 2024 年~2026 年 CAGR 達 26% ， 而高階 CCL 供給端 CAGR
則是 7%，高階市場需求殷切。高階銅箔基板(包含 AI 伺服器和高
階網路交換器，自 2024 年始採用 M8 CCL 材料；2026 年 M8 材料
佔總體 CCL 市場 40%，M8 材料佔比顯著高於 2022 年、2023 年的
22%和 26%。台系銅箔基板供應鏈當中，M6~M8 等高階材料的供
應廠商為(2383)台光電為首、(6274)台燿次之。回顧 3 年來，台燿
產能擴充進程，端視泰國廠進度而定。4Q26 泰國新廠產能陸續開
出，台燿總產能為 260 萬張/月，+13%YoY。展望 2027 年，台燿泰
國廠、常熟廠初步先提升 30 萬張/月，逐步提升總新增產能達 120
萬張/月，預估 2027 年底時，總產能達 380 萬張/月，+46%YoY；預
估 2028 年底時，台燿總產能擴充至 530 萬張/月，+39.5%YoY，平
均 3 年每年產能擴充約 33% 。高階 CCL 在 AI 伺服器、高速網路交
投資建議：買進

換器(高階 Switch)帶動之下，一旦，泰國廠產能擴充可望使得台燿
市佔率得以提升 。 2027 年隨著 NVIDIA Rubin 平台將帶動 CCL M8+ 、
M9 材料出貨攀升，AI ASIC CCL 拉貨動能呈現 50~60%高度成長，
同業台光電與台燿高階 CCL 新產能釋出 ， 產品組合持續優化 。 2027
年起 ， 除一般型伺服器 Intel Diamond Rapids 平台 、 AMD Zen6 Venice
平台採用 PCIe Gen6 與 Ultra Low Loss CCL(M7)，PCB 板層數增至
20~24L；2027 年，隨著 1.6T Switch 逐步導入 M8/M9 CCL(HLC 層
數 46~52L、玻纖 Low Dk2/Q 布、銅箔 HVLP4/5) ，預期 CCL 廠商產
品規格顯著升級。

三.  台燿高階網路 Switch 板材、低軌衛星板材、1.6T 光
模塊板材，27 年規格朝向高階型號發展 
展望 2H26，在高階 Switch 升級方面，800G Switch 採用 M8、M9 
CCL 規格，2H26 台燿將新增高階 Switch 客戶，隨著 AI 伺服器、
高階網路交換器產業發展朝向 800G、1.6T Switch 升級趨勢帶動之
下， 
銅箔基板需要更高速傳輸、訊號低損耗之下會陸續採用 CCL M8、
M9 規格。此外，2H26 開始 800G 與 1.6T 光纖收發模組(採用 M8、
M9 CCL)開始放量，AI 資料中心帶動光纖收發模組需求，400G 以
上光纖收發模組出貨量 2026 年 1 億個、2027 年 1.45 億個、2028 年
達到 1.6~1.65 億個；在中系系統廠商積極拉貨之下，1.6T 光纖收
發模組 2026/2027/2028 年估量 15~20M/55~60M/85~100M ， 1.6T 光纖
收發模組，於 2 年內呈倍數增長。低軌大型 S 客戶 V3 版本新型火
箭搭載低軌衛星 ， 所採用之銅箔基板升級至 M8 等級 ， 材料損耗要
求降低，訊號傳出完整性標準提高，對於 CCL、PCB(HDI)、玻纖
與銅箔等規格預期同步升級，推升材料成本與 PCB 產值。

四.預估 2026/2027 年稅後 EPS 38.49/66.22 元 
2026 年受惠 AI 伺服器及 ASIC 需求持續強勁的帶動下，台燿為網
通、高階網路交換器銅箔基板重要供貨廠商，將受惠 AI Server 對
於高階銅箔基板強勁的需求，因而，預估台燿 2026 年營收 618.78
億元，+108.01%YoY，毛利率 31.13%，稅後淨利 114.79 億元，稅後
EPS+38.49元 。 隨泰國廠產能釋出 ， 預估台燿 2027年營收增至 963.61
億元，+55.73%YoY，毛利率為 33.26%，稅後淨利 198.23 億元，稅
後 EPS+66.22 元。

四.投資建議維持買進，目標價 2,150 元 
宏遠投顧認為，台燿營運重點在於，營運動能於 2026 年起加速啟
動，一般型伺服器 CPU 廠商 Intel 、AMD 均有新品帶動新平台升級
與相關供應的零組件商機，以及攻入 AI 伺服器、AI 高階網路交換

貳、公 司 概 況

一. 公司簡介 
台燿(6274)為銅箔基板廠商，主要產製雙面印刷電路板與多層印刷
電路板之銅箔基板 、 內層銅箔基板 、 黏合片 ， 產品應用別當中 ， ELL
與 SLL(M7、M8)佔比 39%、VLL 與 LL(M4、M6)佔 31%、HDI 與 N-
LL 佔 16%、其他品項佔 14%。

二.2026~2027 年營運概況及展望 
1.HDI 與 M8 以上升級仍為關注焦點，2026~2028 年產能建置積極 
過往一般型伺服器用板採用 HDI 設計，強調細線路設計，CPU 尺
寸變大 Pin Count 數量增加至 2,000 只~4,000 只 Pin Count，PCB 板
面積不變之下要走向細線路化(線寬/線距)朝向更細線路設計。製
程上 HDI 經過多次壓合後，PP 在熱壓多次後，每一層熱膨脹係數
不同，導致通孔 Crack 或翹曲，製作時容易產生爆板，因此對於
PCB 板廠製作良率會是一大技術難關，而金像電、健鼎等廠商於
高階 HDI 相對具備生產優勢與競爭力，相對應台光電、台燿等高
階 CCL 供貨與升級也成為製程一大瓶頸；回顧 3 年來，台燿產能
擴充進程，端視泰國廠進度而定。4Q26 泰國新廠產能陸續開出，
台 燿 總 產 能 為 260 萬 張 /月，+13%YoY。展 望 2027 年，台 燿 泰 國 廠、
常熟廠初步先提升 30 萬張/月，逐步提升總新增產能達 120 萬張/
月 ， 預估 2027 年底時 ， 總產能達 380 萬張/月 ， +46%YoY ； 預估 2028
年底時，台燿總產能擴充至 530 萬張/月，+39.5%YoY，平均 3 年
每年產能擴充約 33%。高階 CCL 在 AI 伺服器、高速網路交換器
(高階 Switch)帶動之下，一旦，泰國廠產能擴充可望使得台燿市佔
率得以提升。2027 年隨著 Nvidia Rubin 平台將帶動 CCL M8+、M9
材料出貨攀升，AI ASIC CCL 拉貨動能呈現 50~60%高度成長，台
光電與台燿等高階 CCL 新產能釋出，產品組合持續優化。

針對 AI GPU 與 ASIC 發展進程，Google TPU 出貨量從 2026 年 400
萬顆上修至 2027 年(V8 增量)增至 800 萬顆，倍數增長。AWS ASIC 
預計 2027 年出貨量達 330~350 萬顆，增長+21%YoY 。NVIDIA 體系
GPU，Rubin 系列層數提升至 24~26L，材料端從 M8/M8+往更高規
格升級，更高層數與多層式架構，促使 PCB 板訊號走線、供電完
整性、高速訊號之阻抗匹配控制、訊號傳輸完整性，皆帶動 PCB

1 
6426 統新 
(Apogee Optocom Co.)

報告日期： 2026/10/19 研 究 員：方姿婷  (Lesley Fang) 
壹、投 資 建 議 
重點摘要： 
 統新 2Q26 營收為 2.08 億元，+34.7%QoQ、+82.4%YoY，毛利率
躍升至 56.20%，稅後淨利 0.86 億元，單季稅後 EPS+2.22 元。
2Q26 受惠生產效率提升、產能滿載毛利率躍升。 
 AI 伺服器帶動統新的 DWDM 產品延伸至 AI 資料中心 DCI 主要
骨幹濾片，光纖波段分離與光纖傳輸頻寬增加，會需要統新薄
膜濾光片作為訊號傳輸強化。除 Scale-Across 長距離骨幹，分
散式 AI 數據中心，專門做長距離光通訊傳輸 DWDM 市場，
2026~2031 年每年維持 31%年複合成長。 
 1.6T 光纖收發模組 ， 未來 2 年呈倍數增長 。 1.6T 光纖收發模組，
在後端 PD(接收端)改採 200G PD，會用到 8 片統新之薄膜濾光
片。

一. 統新 2Q26 營收與財報優於市場預期，3Q26 續強 
統新 2Q26 營收為 2.08 億元，+34.7%QoQ、+82.4%YoY，毛利率躍
升至 56.20%，稅後淨利 0.86 億元，單季稅後 EPS+2.22 元。主要受
惠於 AI 資料中心互連需求，使得統新產線維持滿載。展望 3Q26
預估統新營收為 2.42 億元，+16.35%QoQ，毛利率 56.58%，稅後淨
利 1.06 億元，單季稅後 EPS+2.76 元。統新預計產能增加 30%，以
因應客戶需求。

二. 2026 年 DWDM 隨 AI 骨幹網絡建置，濾片量增價漲 
統新高密度波長多工器( D W D M，D e n s e  W a v e l e n g t h  D i v i s i o n  
Multiplexing)，至少可傳輸 16 種不同顏色的光纖，最高可傳輸 128
種不同顏色之光纖，同一條主幹網路上，可以傳輸的頻寬因此加
大，相當具有傳輸效率。DWDM 於發送端將不同波長的光信號組
合起來(覆用, MUX)，並耦合到光纜線路上的同一根光纖中，進行
傳輸，在接收端又將多個波長的光信號分開(解覆用, DEMUX)，並
作進一步處理，恢復出原波長信號後送入不同的終端接收器。統
新的 DWDM 產品延伸至 AI 資料中心 DCI 主要骨幹濾片，波段分
離 ( S -B a n d、C - B a n d 傳 輸 核 心、L -B a nd 容 量 擴 充、S -B a nd 未 來 預 留、
U-Band 8nm 用於 OTDR 光纖監控偵測主幹網路是否具有斷點或需
要救援)，統新以單通道 100GHz 去細分 DWDM 濾光片，統新推出
新品，50GHz 間隔濾光片，支援的通道數量提升至 96Channels。 
投資評等：買進 
目標價位：375.00 元

三.  AI 伺服器高速傳輸，帶動光通訊主被動元件量增 
Scale-Across 長距離骨幹，分散式 AI 數據中心，專門做長距離光
通訊傳輸 DWDM 市場，2026~2031 年，DWDM 市場每年維持 31%
年 複合 成長。此 外，受惠 AI 伺服 器傳 輸速 率提升，如：Broadcom、
Marvell 等國際晶片廠商，於 2026 年起已推出 800G /1.6T Switch 晶
片組，等同於 AI 伺服器後端 Ports 支援的光纖通訊傳輸會一併升
級至 800G 與 1.6T 傳輸速率。Yole 報告指出，1.6T 光纖收發模組
出貨量，2026 年/2027 年/2028 年預估量 15~20M/55~60M/85~100M，
1.6T 光纖收發模組 ， 未來 2 年內呈倍數增長 。 1.6T 光纖收發模組，
在後端 PD(接收端)改採 200G PD ， 會用到 8 片統新之薄膜濾光片。
在薄膜濾光片鍍膜層數 ， Scale-Across 與 NPO 所需薄膜濾光片層數
約 150L~200L，未來發展至 CPO 時鍍膜層數會再增加 8L，統新為
此預計多準備 10 條產線預計開發 250L+規格，予台系知名晶圓代
工廠商。統新指出，Scale-Out 濾片鍍膜代工量增加，是替中系系
統廠代工(層數約 100~150L)。Scale-Across 濾片鍍膜層數會大於
200L，甚至高達 250L，目前訂單能見度高，涵蓋 1.)同業產能難以
支應，Cohr 交給統新、2.)中系系統廠商加單、3.)台系網通廠商於
低軌衛星鍍膜(LEO 非光學鍍膜) 。統新毛利率之所以跳升，是先前
客戶給統新的試產量僅 20~30 片，2Q26 起量產量增至 K 級別，出
貨量增加、規模經濟效應之下，單月營收損平點(評估為 0.4~0.5
億元)，2Q26 起月營收顯著跳升至 0.6~0.7 億元。預期此營運動能
至少能見度到 4Q26，但由於 DWDM 交期延長至 6~8 個月，預估
2027 年產業供需偏向緊俏。

四.投資建議：買進，目標價 375 元 
統新競爭優勢在於其生產高品質高效率之薄膜濾光片篩選特定輻
射波段的光學元件，能篩選光的強度、改變光譜成分或限定震動
截面，藉由 CWDM/DWDM 技術使相同數據集中於同一根光纖中 
傳輸，增大傳輸容量進而提高光纖的使用效率，生產良率逐步提
升且具佔有一定之市場地位。預估統新 2026 年營收 8.56 億元，
+75.77%YoY，毛利率 53.43%，稅後淨利 3.32 億元，稅後 EPS 為
+8.63 元。展望 2027 年，隨產能積極擴充之下，預估統新年營收
為 17.02 億元，+98.83%YoY ，毛利率預估為 54.85% ，稅後淨利 5.57
億元，稅後 EPS+14.47 元。考量基礎建設帶動 DWDM 規格升級，
AI 伺服器傳輸速率提升因應 1.6T 傳輸薄膜濾光片用量同步增長，
給予統新投資建議買進，目標價 375 元(26.00 X2027 EPS)。

一.公司簡介

統新(6426)為生產精密光學元件，以及高密度分波多工器(DWDM)
關鍵元件─薄膜濾光片(TFF)之專業廠商。各產品線營收占比為主
動光學元件佔比低於 15%、被動光學元件：80~85%、光學鍍膜元
件與代工低於 10%。主要產品 DWDM 濾光片 (Dense Wavelength 
Division Multiplexer，即為高密度波長多工分工器)技術可以大幅提
高光纖通訊系統之頻寬，成為光纖通訊系統的市場中關注的焦
點。統新國內主要競爭對手為東典光電科技(股)，國外廠商包含
II-IV Cohernet(美國)、Iridian Technologies(加拿大)等廠商投入相關
產品研發及生產。

圖一、光通訊元件

資料來源：公司、宏遠投顧整理

二.26 年營運概況及 26 年展望 
1.統新 DWDM 技術層層堆疊良率優異  
統新主要產品為薄膜濾光片即為篩選特定輻射波段的光學元件，
能篩選光的強度、改變光譜成分或限定震動截面，藉由不同光線
原理而選擇通過的光源(紅、藍、綠光) ，且藉由 CWDM/DWDM 技
術使相同數據集中於同一根光纖中傳輸，增大傳輸容量進而提高
光纖的使用效率。

2.因應 AI 資料中心基建商機，薄膜濾光片產品走向更高規  
回顧 5G 時期的薄膜濾光片，支援頻段增加，切割更細，中心波

長 1267.5nm、1274.5nm 切割更為細窄，至 1374.5nm 從過往支援
波長 6 組延伸至 12 組 ， 每一道光波段間距縮短至 3.5nm 甚至更
短，薄膜濾波濾光片用量增加，設計難度提升。隨著支援頻段愈
切分愈細，波段之間間距愈來愈短，需要用的薄膜濾波片數量倍
增，且每一片薄膜濾波片設計難度提升，包含設計層數 200 層以
上以及過濾效果較佳，而統新於濾波片設計良率優於競爭同業，
可望取得一定市場地位。

Jason.JC.Tsai@Yuanta.com 
陳冠成 
charleschen24@yuanta.com

煉油利差仍佳，預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 上修 4.2%至 2.04 元 
煉油利差亮麗，3Q26 營業利益 320.82 億元，年增 245.6%，稅後 EPS 為
2.76 元，年增 206.3%，獲利創單季新高。4Q26 冬季取暖維繫柴油消費動
能抵銷煉油開工滑落，預估 4Q26營收維持 1939.85億元，季減 20.4%，年
增 34.9%，營益率上修 0.5ppt至 11.9%，營業利益上修 4.4%至 230.55億
元，季減 28.1%，年增 338.4%，稅後 EPS 上修 4.2%至 2.04 元，季減
26.1%，年增 280.3%，4Q26營益率季減係反映汽油消費轉淡，煉油利差收
斂，惟沙烏地 OSP 折價擴大至 5 美元，汽油價差優於預期，因而上修獲利。 
預估 1Q27 稅後 EPS 上修 3.5%至 1.78 元 
展望 1Q27 農曆年假工作天數較少，不過受損的煉油產能難以恢復，冬天取
暖用油需求仍強，柴油供需持續偏緊，維繫價差高檔，預估 1Q27 營收維持
1833.16億元，季減 5.5%，年增 13.2%，營益率上修 0.3ppt至 10.7%，營
業利益上修 2.9%至 196.66億元，季減 14.7%，年減 19.8%，稅後 EPS上
修 3.5%至 1.78元，季減 12.9%，年減 17.0%。獲利年減，乃因美伊戰爭爆
發導致 1Q26基期較高，不過 1Q27獲利仍遠優於 2025年各季表現。 
煉油產能受損難以恢復，供應吃緊，利差展望佳 
煉油產能受損難以恢復，供給吃緊，估 2026 年營收上修 1.1%至 7826.12
億元，年增 25%，稅後 EPS上修 1.2%至 9.12元，年增 780.1%，推估配
息 6.39元，殖利率達 5.2%以上，仍具吸引力。預期 2027年營收 7559.99
億元，年減 3.4%，加計南亞科現金股利挹注，稅後 EPS上修 10.9%至 6.84
元，雖年減 25.1%，遠優於 2025年獲利低迷狀態。目前本淨比 2.17倍，處
於歷史本淨比 1.0倍-3.0倍區間中間，重申買進建議，目標價由 125元上調
至 160元，本淨比由 2.2倍上修至 2.8倍及 2027年預估每股淨值 56.36元
推得，本淨比上修係反映煉油利差亮麗，提振獲利，評價上修。 
元大觀點 
⧫  
3Q26 稅後 EPS 2.76 元，年增 206.3%，創單季新高。而煉油利差仍
佳，預估 4Q26/2026 稅後 EPS 上修 4.2%/1.2%至 2.04 元/9.12 元。 
⧫  
柴油價差維持高檔，且加計南亞科股利收入挹注， 預估 1Q27/2027 年
稅後 EPS 各上修 3.5%/10.9%至 1.78 元/6.84 元。 
⧫  
煉油產能受損 ，成品油供應吃緊， 提振利差，重申買進，目標價 上調
至 160 元，基於 2.8 倍本淨比及 2027 年估每股淨值 56.36 元推得。

### Page 2

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：塑化 2026年 10月 19日 FPCC 第2 頁，共 18頁
營運分析
煉油利差亮麗，台塑化 3Q26 稅後 EPS 續增至 2.76 元，季增 26.5%，年增
206.3%，優於預期 1.1%
台塑化(6505)公布 3Q26自結獲利續揚，動能亮麗。3Q26營收 2436.54億元(原估 2351.33億元)，
季增 33.2%，年增 49%，優於預期 3.6%，儘管成品油均價季跌 4.7%至 128.9美元，小幅回檔，
不過原油供應恢復，開工率季增 24.7ppt至 91.2%，成品油銷售量季增 43.7%至 4832.3萬桶，帶
動煉油營收季增 35%，至於烯烴事業營收季增 17%，乃係原料供應增加，開工率季增 6.5ppt 至
39.6%，銷售量季增 26.1%至 78.3 萬噸，至於公用事業營收季增 12.3%，反映夏季電費較高，及
售電量與售氣量均增溫。
3Q26營益率季增 0.7ppt至 13.17%(原估 13.19%)，符合預期，營業利益 320.82億元(原估 310.15

億元)，季增 40.2%，年增 245.6%，優於預期 3.4%，業外現金股利收入約 6.4億元，稅後 EPS為
2.76元(原估 2.73元)，季增 26.5%，年增 206.3%，優於預期 1.1%，獲利創單季新高。3Q26營益
率回升係因美伊談判陷入膠著，中東煉油產能恢復延後，而俄羅斯煉油廠遭受烏克蘭攻擊，產出下
滑，煉油利差自 6 月低點逐步走高，加上沙烏地阿拉伯調降對亞洲原油的官方售價(OSP)，成本下
降，提振 3Q26煉油利差擴大，加上認列 27.9億元存貨評價利益，帶動整體獲利走高。

### Page 3

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：塑化 2026年 10月 19日 FPCC 第3 頁，共 18頁  
汽油消費轉淡且歲修干擾，惟整體煉油利差仍優於預期，預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 上
修 4.2%至 2.04 元，年增 280.3% 
展望 4Q26 美伊談判陷入膠著，難以擺脫僵局狀態，且沙烏地與葉門胡塞組織戰火擴大，預期中東
局勢仍將處於不穩定狀態，油價延續高檔整理，而原油供應應可處於穩定狀態，且歐洲積極建立柴
油庫存，以滿足冬季取暖需求，有利柴油價格強勢。另一方面，儘管汽油消費進入淡季，不過煉油
廠歲修較為密集，開工率降低，且全球汽油庫存偏低，及中國限制成品油出口，帶動汽油價格 於
10 月回彈，汽油價差優於預期，而煉油基礎單元於 10 月至 11 月進行歲修，煉油開工率由 3Q26
的 91%降至 75%，產銷量下滑，至於烯烴事業營運平淡，預估開工率維持在 35-40%左右。維持
4Q26營收 1939.85億元之預估，季減 20.4%，年增 34.9%，營益率上修 0.5ppt至 11.9%%(原估
11.4%)，營業利益上修 4.4%至 230.55億元(原估 220.85億元)，季減 28.1%，年增 338.4%，稅後
EPS上修 4.2%至 2.04元(原估 1.96元)，季減 26.1%，年增 280.3%，4Q26營益率季減係反映歲
修干擾，稼動率下滑之影響，惟沙烏地阿拉伯對亞洲原油的官方售價(OSP)折價幅度擴大至 5 美元，
成本下降，整體獲利仍優於原先預期，台塑化 4Q26獲利表現，將遠優於 4Q25同期。

預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 上修 1.2%至 9.12 元，年增 780.1% 
展望 2026 年，美伊戰爭引發的中東局勢動盪可能往常態化發展，中東產能運作受衝擊，且俄羅斯
煉油廠遭受烏克蘭攻擊，產出下滑，而歐美老舊煉油廠相繼關廠退出市場，中國亦有淘汰老舊煉油
廠產能規劃，供給受限，而美國恐限制柴油出口，成品油供給吃緊，而成品油需求穩健，維繫煉油
利差。台塑化獲利回溫，預估 2026 年營收上修 1.1%至 7826.12 億元(原估 7740.90 億元)，年增
25%，營益率上修 0.1ppt 至 13.1%(原估 13.0%)，營業利益上修 2.0%至 1025.35 億元(原估
1004.97億元)，年增 842.5%，稅後 EPS上修 1.2%至 9.12元(原估 9.01元)，年增 780.1%，年底
淨值 55.91元(原估 55.80元)。

### Page 4

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：塑化 2026年 10月 19日 FPCC 第4 頁，共 18頁  
預估 1Q27 稅後 EPS 上修 3.5%至 1.78 元，年減 17%  
展望 1Q27 農曆年假工作天數較少，成品油產出下滑，不過美伊和談進展不順，中東政局動盪，預
期油價延續高檔格局，中東及俄羅斯煉油產能受損情況則難以快速恢復，柴油供給受限，至於需求
端，冬天取暖用油需求仍強，預期柴油供需仍將延續緊俏短缺格局。有利柴油價格及價差延續強勢，
預估 1Q27營收維持 1833.16億元，季減 5.5%，年增 13.2%，營益率上修 0.3ppt至 10.7%(原估
10.4%)，營業利益上修 2.9%至 196.66億元(原估 191.16億元)，季減 14.7%，年減 19.8%，稅後
EPS上修 3.5%至 1.78元(原估 1.72元)，季減 12.9%，年減 17.0%。1Q27獲利年減，乃因美伊戰
爭爆發，推動 2026 年 3 月之後成品油價格飆漲，價差擴大，墊高基期所致，不過獲利仍遠優於
2025年各季表現。

預估 2027 年稅後 EPS 上修 10.9%至 6.84 元，年減 25.1% 
展望 2027 年，OPEC+增產原油恐壓抑油價，不過中東局勢動盪常態化，加上 OECD 國家回補原
油庫存，將支撐油價，預期油價延續偏高檔整理，惟預期中東及俄羅斯受損的煉油產能可能逐步恢
復運作，煉油利差將較 2026 年滑落，但仍會優於美伊戰爭前的水準，預估 2027 年營收維持
7559.99億元，年減 3.4%，營益率上修 0.1ppt至 9.1%(原估 9.0%)，年減 4.0ppt，營業利益上修
0.8%至 684.10 億元(原估 678.60 億元)，年減 33.3%，業外方面，加計持有南亞(1303)及南亞科
(2408)預期將配發之現金股利合計推估約 72.7億元之挹注，稅後 EPS上修 10.9%至 6.84元(原估
6.17元)，年減 25.1%，年底淨值 56.36元(原估 55.66元)。 
若以 70%股利配發率計算，推估 2027年台塑化將配息 6.39元，以目前股價計算，殖利率達 5.2%
以上，仍具吸引力。

### Page 5

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：塑化 2026年 10月 19日 FPCC 第5 頁，共 18頁  
中東煉油產能受損及俄羅斯煉油廠受攻擊，維繫煉油價差表現，提振台塑化獲利 
煉油事業營運方面，2026 年 2 月底美伊戰爭爆發，驅動油價大漲，成品油價格也隨之走高，其中
柴油報價自 2月底約 90美元一路走高至 4月上旬的 220美元以上，柴油價差也自 14美元擴大至
140美元，汽油報價則自 2月底約 77美元一路走高至 3月下旬 160美元以上，汽油價差自 1美元
擴大至 48 美元，隨著美伊於 4月啟動和談，油價處於高檔震盪，成品油價格也同樣處於高位，柴
油價差則自 4月初高點收斂至 50-60美元區間，汽油價差則收斂至 20-30美元區間，2026/6/12美
伊雙方針對停火條件達成共識，並於 6/17正式簽署包含 14個要點諒解備忘錄 MOU，油價及成品
油價格見到進一步修正，柴油價差收斂至 30-35美元區間，汽油價差收斂至 15-20美元。 
7月以來美伊戰爭再起，油價大漲，成品價格隨之走高，價差再度擴大，柴油價差擴大至 50-60美
元，汽油價差擴大至 25-30美元，8月及 9月柴油價差擴大至 60-70美元，10月持續維持在 60美
元以上，汽油價差於 8月及 9月維持在 20-25美元之間，10月則突破 30美元以上，優於預期，汽
油價差擴大乃因全球汽油產能歲修較為密集，開工率降低，且全球汽油庫存偏低，以及中國限制成
品油出口，帶動汽油價格回彈。儘管柴油及汽油價差自 4月初的高點收斂，不過對比美伊戰前，柴
油價差多處於 15-25美元區間，汽油價差多處於 10-15美元，近期柴油及汽油價差仍高於美伊戰前
水準。另一方面，基於 OPEC+決議原油增產，市場競爭加劇，沙烏地阿拉伯則調降對亞洲原油的
官方售價(OSP)，6月為溢價 15.5美元，7月為溢價 9.5美元，8月降至折價 1.5美元，9月折價
2.0美元，10月延續折價 2.0美元，11月折價幅度再擴大至 5.0美元，推估 12月仍將維持折價狀
態，亞洲國家原油採購成本降低，可維繫煉油利差處於高檔水準。展望後市，由於中東煉油設備受
損，成品油產能仍須一段時間才有機會逐漸恢復，且俄羅斯煉油廠遭受烏克蘭攻擊，產出下滑，成
品油供給相對偏緊，美國川普總統施壓歐洲國家釋出柴油庫存，顯示供需缺口擴大，預期柴油及汽
油價差可維持高於美伊戰前水準，有利台塑化煉油事業獲利維持亮麗表現，較 2025年大幅改善。

南亞科(2408)股價波動劇烈，先保守以 2Q26 底收盤價作為評價基礎，推估台塑化
2026 年底淨值 55.91 元 
台塑化藉由”透過其他綜合損益按公允價值衡量之金融資產 -非流動”會計科目持有南亞科(2408)股權
約 10.81%，每季底依據南亞科股價變動造成的持股市值變化將反映在台塑化之股權權益及淨值。
台塑化分別於 2025/10/28、11/11及 12/31公告相繼已處分 2.5萬、1.8萬及約 1.9萬張南亞科持
股，平均處分價格各為 98.67元、145.09元及 169.46元，台塑化目前持有南亞科股權降至 8.81%。
簡單試算，若南亞科股價每變動 10元，將造成台塑化淨值變動約 0.29元。 
若以南亞科最新 (2026/10/17) 股價 447.5元計算，對比 2026/6/30收盤價 452.5元，台塑化持股
8.81%的評價利益將較 2Q26底減少約 13.64億元，將導致台塑化淨值減少約 0.14元。由於南亞科
股價波動較為劇烈，先保守以 2026/6/30 收盤價作為評價基礎，推估台塑化 2026 年底淨值為
55.91元(原估 55.80元) ，2026年淨值大幅提升乃係反映持股南亞科股價大漲後的評價利益貢獻。

產業概況  
美伊和談進展反覆，油價於 6 月大幅下挫超過 30%後，3Q26 大幅反彈 
美國與伊朗於 2026年 4月初開始啟動停火協議談判，經歷過 2個多月斡旋及拉鋸，於 2026/6/12
雙方針對停火條件達成共識，並於 6/17正式簽署包含 14個要點諒解備忘錄 MOU，承諾在 60天內
達成最終全面協議。在美伊進行停火談判期間，荷姆茲海峽仍處於封鎖狀態，全球原油供應仍有短
缺，不過 OECD 主要國家以原油庫存支應需求，加上美伊和談的預期心理，油價即面臨震盪拉回
的修正壓力，期間美伊談判反覆幾度再推動油價反彈，但都未能突破 3月高點，呈現高檔整理格局，
美伊雙方談判一度進展順利，油價於 5月初高點 100美元以上，一路修正至 6月下旬波段低點，西
德州原油跌破 70 美元，布蘭特原油跌破 75 美元，波段跌幅均超過 30%，走勢疲弱，已跌至
2026/2/28 美伊戰爭爆發前的油價區間 65-70 美元。3Q26 以來，美伊談判陷入反覆，並再次交火，
油價再反彈回升至 100美元以上。近期美伊戰火停息，美國計畫改採對伊朗經濟與貿易制裁手段，
美伊雙方對和談條件的歧見仍深，短期難以達成和談共識，談判陷入膠著，油價將呈現高檔震盪格
局。 
圖 6：西德州與布蘭特原油價格走勢圖(美元/桶)

資料來源：Platts、元大投顧整理

美國釋出戰備庫存壓制油價，亦可望於低檔回補庫存，預期油價上檔壓力區 100-
110 美元，下檔支撐區 70-75 美元 
另一方面，觀察 OECD 國家原油庫存趨勢，美伊戰爭開打後，各國政府為因應荷姆茲海峽封鎖造
成的原油供應短缺，先提供原油庫存滿足原油需求，造成原油庫存於近 4 個月庫存大減 2.49 億桶
至 25.59億桶，為 2024年 2月以來新低，也低於過去 4年平均的 27.7億桶。3Q26油價一度大漲，
為壓制油價漲勢，美國能源部宣布將從戰略石油儲備釋出 4000 萬桶原油，並敦促歐洲成員國釋出
戰備原油儲備，預期 OECD 國家原油庫存恐延續滑落格局。荷姆茲海峽仍有油輪通行，加上替代
油路，中東產油國原油運輸恢復，供應趨於穩定，亦有機會壓制原油價格漲勢，預期油價 100-110
美元區間將是上檔壓力區。至於油價的支撐區研判落在 70-75美元，乃考量各國政府有動機於油價
相對低檔之際，進行原油庫存回補，可為油價形成支撐。

全球煉油產能受損比率約 6.7%，成品油供應受衝擊 
全球煉油產能約 1.03億桶，主要煉油產能集中在亞太地區占比 38.3%最大，其次為北美 20.9%，
歐洲 11.8%，至於中東煉油產能僅約 1120萬桶，占比約 10.9%為第四大煉油地區，美伊戰爭爆發
後，推估中東受損的煉油產能約 540 萬桶，占比約 5.2%。另一方面，烏俄戰爭演變為長期對抗，
烏克蘭無人機精準打擊俄羅斯煉油設備，俄羅斯煉油總產能約 550 萬桶，推估受損產能約 150 萬
桶，占比約 1.46%。若合計中東及俄羅斯受損的煉油產能，合計約 690萬桶，占比約 6.7%，全球
成品油供應受衝擊，也推動柴油及汽油價格與利差維持高檔。

圖 8：全球煉油產能分布圖

### Page 9

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：塑化 2026年 10月 19日 FPCC 第9 頁，共 18頁  
亞洲廠商缺料減產的乙烯產能損失缺口約 16-17%，供需過剩格局趨於改善 
荷姆茲海峽封鎖期間，原油運輸中斷，高度依賴中東原料供應的亞洲煉油及石化廠商面臨缺料狀況，
包括日本、韓國、台灣、中國及東南亞等均有石化廠商相繼宣布不可抗力因素而減產，調降開工率。
以乙烯產能為例，亞洲乙烯產能佔全球比重約 45%，若因缺料而將開工率由原本正常的 75-80%水
準調降至 50%左右，推估一個月乙烯產能損失造成的乙烯供需缺口約 12.5%，加計中東約 15%乙
烯產能受損而運作不順(假設開工率僅 20%)，就算美洲(包括美國及南美)的石化廠商會伺機拉高開
工率填補，推估全球乙烯一個月實際的缺口約 16-17%。至於其他基礎石化原料因缺料及運作不順
的產能損失大概落在 14-17%不等。整體而言，缺料造成減產的供給限縮，有助於加速庫存去化，
塑化產品供需過剩格局趨於改善。

圖 9：全球石化基礎原料因缺料減產的產能損失推估比率彙整表

資料來源：元大投顧整理

中東受損的石化產能需要時間修復，有利石化產品價格及利差回穩 
美伊停火談判導致近 2026年 6月油價回落重挫，而下游產品買氣縮手，市場陷入觀望，拖累塑化
產品報價同步修正。美伊和談反覆，戰火再起，油價止跌回穩，不過荷姆茲海峽仍有油輪通行，加
上替代油路，原油供應逐漸恢復，亞洲石化廠商缺料緩解，開工率可望逐步提升，產出增加，另外
油價回升，有助於強化塑化產品報價的漲價預期，可止跌回穩。中長期而言，美伊戰爭引發的中東
局勢動盪可能往常態化發展，佔全球石化產能比重約 15%的中東地區，因戰爭受損運作停擺之產
能需要較長時間的修復，實質衝擊影響將擴大，預期供給端擴張幅度可控，有利塑化產品報價及利
差回穩，高於 2026年初美伊戰前水準，看法維持正向。

獲利調整與股票評價
煉油產能受損難以恢復，供應吃緊，利差展望佳，獲利上修
展望 2026 年，美伊戰爭引發的中東局勢動盪可能往常態化發展，中東產能運作受衝擊，且俄羅斯
煉油廠遭受烏克蘭攻擊，產出下滑，而歐美老舊煉油廠相繼關廠退出市場，中國亦有淘汰老舊煉油
廠產能規劃，供給受限，而美國恐限制柴油出口，成品油供給吃緊，而成品油需求穩健，維繫煉油
利差。台塑化獲利回溫，預估 2026 年營收上修 1.1%至 7826.12 億元(原估 7740.90 億元)，年增
25%，營益率上修 0.1ppt 至 13.1%(原估 13.0%)，營業利益上修 2.0%至 1025.35 億元(原估
1004.97億元)，年增 842.5%，稅後 EPS上修 1.2%至 9.12元(原估 9.01元)，年增 780.1%，年底
淨值 55.91元(原估 55.80元)。
展望 2027 年，OPEC+增產原油恐壓抑油價，不過中東局勢動盪常態化，加上 OECD 國家回補原

油庫存，將支撐油價，預期油價延續偏高檔整理，惟預期中東及俄羅斯受損的煉油產能可能逐步恢
復運作，煉油利差將較 2026 年滑落，但仍會優於美伊戰爭前的水準，預估 2027 年營收維持
7559.99億元，年減 3.4%，營益率上修 0.1ppt至 9.1%(原估 9.0%)，年減 4.0ppt，營業利益上修
0.8%至 684.10 億元(原估 678.60 億元)，年減 33.3%，業外方面，加計持有南亞(1303)及南亞科
(2408)預期將配發之現金股利合計推估約 72.7億元之挹注，稅後 EPS上修 10.9%至 6.84元(原估
6.17元)，年減 25.1%，年底淨值 56.36元(原估 55.66元)。若以 70%股利配發率計算，推估 2027
年台塑化將配息 6.39元，以目前股價計算，殖利率達 5.2%以上，仍具吸引力。
對台塑化看法正向，建議買進，目標價由 125元上調至 160元。考量：1)成品油供給吃緊，提振煉
油利差，3Q26營業利益 320.82億元，年增 245.6%，稅後 EPS為 2.76元，年增 206.3%，獲利

創單季新高。2) 沙烏地與葉門胡塞組織戰火擴大，油價高檔，而冬季取暖的柴油需求持續強勁，汽
油庫存偏低，中國限制出口，推動汽油價差擴大，4Q26營業利益上修 4.4%至 230.55億元，年增
338.4%，稅後 EPS 上修 4.2%至 2.04 元，年增 280.3%。預估 2026 年營業利益上修 2.0%至
1025.35億元，年增 842.5%，稅後 EPS上修 1.2%至 9.12元，年增 780.1%，擺脫 2025年獲利低
迷狀態。3)推估 2027年台塑化將配息 6.39元，以目前股價計算，殖利率達 5.2%以上，仍具吸引
力。4)受惠轉投資南亞科及南亞現金股利之貢獻，2027年稅後 EPS上修 10.9%至 6.84元。推估
台塑化 2027年底淨值增至 56.36元，目前本淨比 2.17倍，處於歷史本淨比 1.0倍-3.0倍的區間中
間，重申買進建議，目標價由 125元上調至 160元，本淨比由 2.2倍上修至 2.8倍及 2027年預估
每股淨值 56.36元推得，本淨比上修係反映煉油利差亮麗，提振獲利，評價上修。

公司簡介  
台塑石化股份有限公司(6505.TW)成立於 1992 年，由台塑、南亞、台化等公司共同出資成立，為國內唯一之民營石油煉製
業者，且石油製品及石化原料產能規模位居國內第一。另有合格汽電共生系統，供應台塑企業麥寮廠區各工廠所需之蒸汽、
電力等各項公用流體。公司主要業務分為油品及煉油、烯烴事業。油品銷售方面，主要以國內市場需求為優先， 2025 年銷
貨佔比為台灣 37%、歐洲 14%、新加坡 10%、英國 6%和其他 33%，汽柴油在國內市占率約 22%。塑化事業方面，乙烯
在國內市占率約 64%。主要客戶為台塑、台化、南亞、國喬、台橡、台達化等國內下游石化廠。

ESG 分析  
分別藉由「在 ESG議題上的曝險」和「個別公司在 ESG議題上的執行力」兩個面向來衡量公司於 ESG上的表現。 
► ESG 總分： 台塑化整體的 ESG風險評級屬於嚴重風險，於 Sustainalytics資料庫所覆蓋的公司中排名亦排行於落後的
位置，亦於石油天然氣煉製行銷行業的公司中排名落後同業。 
► 在 ESG 議題上的曝險： 台塑化的整體曝險較高，且略高於該石油天然氣煉製行銷行業的平均水準。在 ESG（環境、社
會與治理）方面，值得關注的實質議題包括：產品與服務、排放、廢水與廢棄物，以及職業健康與安全。  
► 個別公司在 ESG 議題上的執行力：台塑化在針對重大 ESG議題的管理水準和執行力為平均水準。該公司展現出堅定的
ESG 承諾，制定了完善的環境政策，並發布了符合國際永續發展標準的、高品質的 ESG 報告。公司透過董事會或高階
主管層面的委員會（或指定成員）強化了對 ESG 事務的監督。然而，其舉報人機制較為薄弱，顯示出該領域亟需改進。

註：A = 未調整之收盤價；B = 未調整之目標價； C = 依據股本變動調整後之目標價。員工分紅稀釋影響未反映於 A、B 或 C。

投資評等說明 
買進：根據本中心對該檔個股投資期間絕對或相對報酬率之預測，我們對該股持正面觀點。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股之發展前景、財務表現、利多題
材、評價資訊以及風險概況之分析。建議投資人於投資部位中增持該股。 
持有-超越同業：本中心認為根據目前股價，該檔個股基本面吸引力高於同業。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股發展前景、財務表現、利多題材、評價資訊
以及風險概況之分析。 
持有-落後同業：本中心認為根據目前股價，該檔個股基本面吸引力低於同業。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股發展前景、財務表現、利多題材、評價資訊
以及風險概況之分析。 
賣出：根據本中心對該檔個股投資期間絕對或相對報酬率之預測，我們對該股持負面觀點。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股之發展前景、財務表現、利多題
材、評價資訊以及風險概況之分析。建議投資人於投資部位中減持該股。 
評估中：本中心之預估、評等、目標價尚在評估中，但仍積極追蹤該個股。 
限制評等：為遵循相關法令規章及/或元大之政策，暫不給予評等及目標價。

南電 (8046 TT) 
ABF 報價優於預期，獲利再度上修

永豐觀點 
南電 ABF 報價漲幅優於預期，2027 年獲利翻倍上修，長線 ABF 供給吃緊至 2028
年態勢不變，獲利延續性可期。 
 投資評價與建議 
南電 3Q26 營收達成率 125%，暗示 ABF 報價漲幅優於預期，長線GPU、ASIC 以
及 CPU 在 AI 需求推升下趨勢向上，南電因報價以短約為主於漲價週期更能快速
反應獲利，研究部上調 2026-2028 年稅後 EPS 至 18.90 元、50.93 元以及 76.51
元，投資建議維持買進，目標價反映獲利上調至 1660 元(26X2027-2028 年平均
EPS)。

ESG 評析 
南電企業永續評鑑整體為 A 等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面向平
均得分分別為 A 等與 A 等。 
SinoPac+ ESG 評鑑系統評等

資料來源：SinoPac+ ESG 評等 (依循 2023 SinoPac+企業永續評鑑方法學)

專業意見，對於評分結果及評估報告內容之完整性及真實性，不負擔保責任，亦不對閱讀或使用

### Page 2

2

南電為專業 ABF 製造商 ：南電為國內專業 IC 載板製造商 ，1H26 產品別 PC(17%)、
Networking(49%)、Consumer(12%)、Auto(6%)以及 AI&HPC(16%)。下游客戶
為 AMD、Broadcom、Hisilicon、Samsung、Continental 以及 Delphi 等，主要
競爭對手為 Ibiden、Samsung、Shinko、欣興以及景碩。 
3Q26 營收達成率 125%，ABF 報價漲幅優於預期：南電 3Q26 營收 163.78 億元
(+20.6%QoQ，+49.3%YoY)，整體 3Q26 營收達成率 125%優於預期，另一方面
日前公布的 8 月自結稅後淨利亦優於預期 3 個百分點，暗示 ABF 報價漲幅優於預
期，研究部將2H26 報價漲幅由原先的 5-10%上調至 10-20%，研究部上調 3Q26
毛利率至 30.4%，稅後淨利 36.60 億元(+61.8%QoQ，+405.0%YoY)，單季稅後
EPS 上調至 5.66 元(原估 3.31 元)。 
ABF 供給吃緊延續，4Q26 營收 QoQ+13.3%：展望 4Q26，研究部認為 ABF 在
AI Server 高速運算、異質整合以及 Agentic AI 帶動 CPU 相關需求拉升下，供給
吃緊態勢延續，預期報價仍具 10-20%漲幅空間，上調單季營收預估值至 185.53
億元 (+13.3%QoQ ， +66.2%YoY)，毛利率 36.3% ， 稅 後 淨 利49.82 億元
(+36.1%QoQ，+314.6%YoY)，單季稅後 EPS 上調至 7.71 元(原估 3.88 元)。 
2H26 ABF 報價優於預期，2026 年獲利上修 48.7%：展望 2026 年，研究部原預
期 ABF 2H26 單季漲幅 5-10%，惟受惠需求優於預期，報價漲幅上調 5-10 個百
分點，搭配整體產業稼動率維持高檔，相關族群獲利具上修空間，考量南電產品主
要走短約更易反映供需，另一方面公司 ABF 營收占比亦達 60%以上，在報價優於
預期時對獲利反映更加敏感，研究部上調全年營收至 596.83 億元(+48.6%YoY)，
毛利率由 20.4%上調至 28.2%，稅後淨利 122.13 億元(+527.3%YoY)，稅後 EPS 
18.90 元(原估 12.71 元)。

營運現況與分析

Agentic AI 助攻，ABF 需求更具延續性：過去 ABF 需求主要聚焦 AI Server 所帶
動的 GPU 及 ASIC 需求，隨著 Agentic AI 大量導入，LLM 於多工處理時 CPU 資
源耗用增加，同步帶動相關需求提升，根據 AMD 於 2Q26 法說預期相關 CPU 產
值將由 2026 年的 600 億美金上升至 2030 年的 2000 億美金，研究部認為在 AI
產品週期由 GPU、ASIC 擴散至 CPU 下，ABF 需求將更具延續性，搭配指標大廠
Ibiden(4026 JP)於日前法說訊息，ABF 至 2028 年仍供給吃緊。 
ABF 供給吃緊可望延續 ， 南電2027-2028 年獲利翻倍上修 ：Nittobo 獨供的 Low 
CTE 玻纖布在 AI Server 用 ABF core layer 板厚增加 50%，造成產能供給吃緊，
連帶排擠 BT 載板中 AP 及 memory 相關供應，研究部預期即便 3Q27 新產能開
出仍不足支應目前 AI Server 殷切需求，ABF 供給吃緊態勢不變。研究部上修南電
2027-2028 年 獲 利 預 估 ，2027 年 營 收 上 修 至39.8% 來到 914.11 億元
(+53.2%YoY)，毛利率 48.3%，稅後淨利 329.08 億元(+169.5%YoY)，稅後 EPS
上修 135.2%至 50.93 元。2028 年營收上修至 1212.26 億元(+32.6%YoY)，毛利
率 53.0%，稅後淨利 494.41 億元(+50.2%YoY)，稅後 EPS 76.51 元(原估 42.03
元)，整體獲利趨勢仍呈高速成長。

## Document 239: Business Checkpoint_ Looking into the logistics Profitability growth was positive in the third quarter, but logistics costs and supply chain pressures are creating challenges. 2 mi

Source PDF: all report pdf/Business Checkpoint_ Looking into the logistics Profitability growth was positive in the third quarter, but logistics costs and supply chain pressures are creating challenges. 2 min read.pdf
Report source: Bank of America
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

13028181     1

Economy 
Business Checkpoint: Looking into the logistics 
15 October 2026

Key takeaways 
• Small and mid-sized businesses entered Q3 in relatively solid financial condition, with profitability growth up across both 
groups, according to Bank of America business account data. Nearly half of estimated tariff payments had been refunded by 
September, though the share of these refunds relative to revenue has peaked. 
• In Bank of America business payments data, logistics spending growth per client was up four times as much for small than mid-
sized firms. Additionally, distribution and inventory transactions growth per client has outpaced spending since the spring of 
2025. 
• Growing transportation bottlenecks are re-emerging ahead of the holiday season. Trucking capacity has tightened as driver 
availability remains constrained, according to BofA Global Research. This has pushed freight rates higher and driven strong 
transportation sector payroll growth, particularly among mid-sized businesses, according to Bank of America payments data.

Business Checkpoint is a new, regular publication from Bank of America Institute. It provides a real-time view of the financial health, opportunities 
and challenges facing small and mid-sized businesses across the US economy, leveraging the depth and breadth of Bank of America's proprietary 
data. By analyzing activity among both small and mid-sized firms, Business Checkpoint offers timely insights into business profitability, hiring, 
investment and spending trends. Such data is not intended to be reflective or indicative of, and should not be relied upon as, the results of operations, 
financial condition or performance of Bank of America. 
Businesses end Q3 in fair shape 
While profitability growth remained positive for both small and mid-sized firms in the third quarter, small firms outpaced their 
mid-sized counterparts for the second consecutive quarter (Exhibit 2). Still, there’s been a notable reversal in the profitability of 
mid-sized businesses, growing this quarter after declining slightly for the past two.  
The continued rollout of tariff refunds is serving as a tailwind to some firms' cash positions. That is particularly true for small 
firms. According to Bank of America account data, by September approximately 47% of estimated International Emergency 
Economic Powers Act-related tariff payments had been refunded to companies with less than $50 million in annual revenue.

According to Bank of America business payments data, the percentage point difference in tariff refunds as a share of revenue 
for firms with less than $50 million in annual revenue spiked in June but dropped to nearly zero in September. At the same time, 
the percentage point difference in tariff payments as a share of expenses began rising again relative to the 2023 average 
(Exhibit 1). This suggests that while tariffs continue to affect companies, the refund program has provided temporary relief for 
some businesses.  
Wholesale trade along with transportation and warehousing posted solid profitability growth across business size 
However, the benefits appear uneven. Such refunds have boosted cash flow, but they have not fully insulated firms from broader 
cost pressures.  
Looking across industries, mid-sized construction and wholesale firms had the strongest profitability growth in September 
(Exhibit 3). Notably, transportation and warehousing, as well as wholesale trade were among the few sectors showing positive 
profitability growth for both small and mid-sized firms last month. 
The persistence of logistics, freight and inventory-related cost pressures may help explain why profitability gains have remained 
relatively concentrated in transportation and wholesale trade. However, these sectors also have a higher exposure to rising gas 
prices within their margins (read more on this in the August Small Business Checkpoint). 
Exhibit 3: For both small and mid-sized businesses, profitability growth was positive in transportation and warehousing as well as wholesale trade 
in September 
Profitability growth by sector and business size in September (YoY%, six-month moving average)

BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

The cost of maintaining supply chains is rising 
In fact, the prices paid component of the September services reading from the Institute for Supply Management indicates still-
building price pressure for firms (Exhibit 4). This marked the highest reading since July 2022.   
Using Bank of America internal data, we identified payments to logistics and freight companies to assess a key part of supply 
chain costs. In June, logistics and freight payments’ growth per client from small businesses grew nearly 30%, though has 
moderated to 12.7% YoY in September (Exhibit 5). Meanwhile, mid-sized firms’ payments growth in this category was up just 
3.2% YoY in September. 
The divergence suggests logistics costs may be disproportionately affecting smaller firms, who are typically less capable of 
absorbing rising costs than larger firms. Larger companies also often benefit from dedicated logistics contracts and greater 
purchasing power, while smaller ones may be more exposed to spot-market transportation costs and fluctuations in freight 
pricing.

Evidence of more moving parts in companies’ supply chains 
Yet there is increasing evidence that tariffs and geopolitics are adding complexity to small and mid-sized companies’ supply 
chains. 
In Bank of America payments data, average distribution and inventory transaction growth per business client has increased 
steadily since the spring of 2025 despite spending growth in this category falling in September (Exhibit 6). Rising transaction 
growth alongside softer spending growth suggests businesses may be engaging with more suppliers, shipments or inventory 
movements even as overall procurement outlays moderates.  
Furthermore, in May 2026, the Global Supply Chain Pressure Index reached its highest reading since the summer of 2022 and has 
continued to remain elevated into October (Exhibit 7). This further emphasizes that transportation bottlenecks, delays and 
procurement challenges are becoming more common globally. While current readings remain below the extreme levels observed 
during the pandemic, the recent move higher indicates supply chain normalization may be reversing.

The trials of trucking  
As of October 1, BofA Global Research's proprietary bi-weekly Truckload Demand Indicator (see Methodology) remained below 
its historical growth signal (Exhibit 9) and the Truck Capacity Indicator, which reflects shippers' views of available capacity, 
tightened significantly, falling from 39.2 to 28.4 (Exhibit 8). This suggests shippers are seeing fewer trucks available relative to 
demand than in the previous survey. 
According to BofA Global Research, the current trucking upcycle is being driven by constrained supply rather than a significant 
improvement in freight demand. While any positive movement in demand is an important indicator as the industry moves into 
peak shipping season between late September and early December, tighter capacity appears to be the dominant force 
supporting freight rates.

The reduction in capacity reflects several factors, including stricter commercial driver's license (CDL) and training requirements, 
increased compliance and enforcement measures, and other regulatory changes that have reduced the pool of qualified drivers. 
This has also likely driven some wage inflation within the industry. According to Bank of America payments data, transportation 
and warehousing payroll growth per client has surged since the start of the year, especially for mid-sized businesses (Exhibit 10).  
Having to pay higher wages suggests labor shortages remain a challenge in the sector. Firms may increasingly need to offer 
higher compensation to attract and retain workers as trucking capacity tightens. In turn, such capacity puts upward pressure on 
freight rates, particularly in the spot market where small businesses may be more impacted.

Logistics payments tend to increase in the second half of the year 
September marks a key run-up to the holiday season (read more on signs of holiday spending in the October Consumer 
Checkpoint), with spending on ocean shipping, containers, and other transportation services steadily accelerating ahead of the 
peak shopping period.  
In fact, over the past two years, the month of November has seen ocean shipping/container spending volumes well above their 
2023-2025 monthly average (Exhibit 11). The seasonal pattern likely reflects inventory restocking and holiday-related shipping 
activity. Ocean shipping volumes typically accelerate during the fall months as businesses prepare for year-end demand.

Distributors and inventory payments’ growth fell ahead of holiday season 
Changes in logistics and freight spending often provide an early signal of inventory trends, as distributors account for a large 
share of supply chain-related spending (Exhibit 12). 
The sharp moderation in distribution and inventory spending growth per client for both small and mid-sized businesses indicates 
that much of the large-scale inventory accumulation seen earlier in 2025 has already occurred (Exhibit 13). In other words, 
businesses appear to be replenishing inventories for seasonal demand rather than significantly expanding stockpiles as they did 
earlier in the year. 
Overall, businesses seem to be entering the holiday season in relatively stable financial condition, supported by tariff refunds 
and positive profitability growth. However, rising logistics costs, tighter trucking capacity and elevated supply chain pressures 
suggest operating conditions are becoming more challenging.

Methodology 
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business and/or Business Banking clients with a deposit account or a 
Small Business credit card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of 
America per Business client data represents activity spending from active Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card and at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and are 
generally defined as under $5mm in annual sales revenue. Mid-sized businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America 
and are generally defined as $5mm-$50mm in annual sales revenue. 
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
Revenue tiers are determined by the combination of following factors: 1) stated revenue on small businesses credit applications, 2) actual 
account inflow into Bank of America Deposit Accounts, and 3) third party revenue estimation.  
The alternative hiring indicator consists of payments from Bank of America business clients to business-focused hiring firms which include both 
direct deposits through Automated Clearing House (ACH) and payments via credit and debit cards. 
Supply chain-related spending is comprised of payments to logistics, freight, distribution, inventory, delivery service, and trucking companies. 
These are identified from merchant names and MCC codes. 
BofA Global Research’s trucking indicators are a bi-weekly diffusion-style survey of shipper demand expectations over the next 0–3 months, 
built from responses from surveyed shippers. 
Data regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook
September 14, 2026
All data, projections and opinions are as of the date of this report and subject to change.
IN THIS ISSUE
Macro Strategy—Superintelligence and Superabundance in the Next Decade:
Rapid advances in Artificial Intelligence (AI) have prompted prominent architects of
the new economy to conclude the Singularity is arriving ahead of schedule as signs
of its effects proliferate in the multitude of new AI models running in the increasing

number of data centers driving one of the biggest investment booms in U.S. history.
Superintelligence that surpasses collective human intelligence has long been one of the
predictions of futurists analyzing the implications of the Singularity. Superabundance is
one of the results of Superintelligence predicted by those who have written about the
consequences of this major acceleration in technological innovation. The early arrival of
these radical forces for change poses imminent challenges for society and investors that

will shape the next decade in critical ways.
In particular, the heightened uncertainty about how this new era plays out increases the
utility of a well-diversified approach to wealth management in our view.
Market View—Elevated Debt Supply Is Testing the Market's Appetite: The recent
rise in Treasury yields reflects a confluence of factors, including Federal Reserve (Fed)
policy uncertainty, deteriorating fiscal dynamics, resilient economic growth, persistent

inflation concerns and a record wave of debt issuance from both the U.S. Treasury and
corporate borrowers, particularly in the long end of the curve. Importantly, we view this
as a period of market adjustment rather than a cause for alarm.
Episodes of debt pressures can be a natural consequence of elevated supply, in addition
to other external factors and could be resolved through higher yields that help balance
supply and demand. While interest rate volatility may remain elevated and long-term

yields could settle at structurally higher levels, today's combination of attractive nominal
and real yields presents a potential opportunity for investors to extend beyond cash and
short-duration holdings and move closer to strategic duration targets.

Officer (CIO).

### Page 2

2 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Superintelligence and Superabundance in the Next Decade
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
While some academics have claimed the Singularity is still off in the distant future, or even
unlikely at all, those on the front lines of AI model development have recently asserted that it
has already begun. For example, Elon Musk has pointed to recent issues with AI models going

outside their proscribed limits, including hacking external systems and completing previously
unsolved math problems. Over a year ago, Sam Altman wrote, "We are past the event horizon; the
takeoff has started. Humanity is close to building superintelligence..." In short, the rapid evolution
of these models has convinced knowledgeable observers that the Singularity has begun.
Superintelligence is the primary driver of the Singularity as exponential advances in AI ultimately

create an artificial Superintelligence that is much smarter and faster than the collective
intelligence of humanity. As such, it can solve practical problems that remain unsolved by humans
and do it in a timely fashion. Superabundance is the logical implication of Superintelligence
as it works to create a world where economic goods, services and energy are essentially free
because of the omniscience implied by the most efficient processing of unlimited information by

superintelligent models. In essence, Superintelligence could mimic the Creation.
Some aspects of Superabundance include automated production of desired output that
eliminates most physical labor, clean energy and synthetic materials that eliminate resource
scarcity, and increased longevity as Superintelligence is applied to maintaining physical health
via, for example, synthetic organ replacement and personalized illness treatments. Obviously,
Superabundance pulls the rug out from under traditional economics, which is the study of how

scarce resources are allocated. While the "scarcity" vanishes in this new world, the allocation issue
remains and is likely to become the focus of political debate. In particular, the question of how
jobs and incomes fit into a superabundant world is likely to become a bigger and bigger issue.
The trend of technology replacing more and more of the labor share of income is already decades
old and dates back to the beginnings of digital technology. That trend has picked up speed

with the increasing profit share of output the new technologies created. The ratio of income
to the number of workers is orders of magnitude higher for the big new technology companies
compared to the old economy companies of the past. This means the owners of capital
(shareholders) are receiving a bigger share of the economic pie as high-margin, high-productivity
companies based in the new technology become a bigger part of the economy. This trend is

accelerating. That is a direct implication of the movement toward and through the Singularity.
It means the issue of income inequality, which has always been on society's radar screen, will
become bigger in the years ahead unless there are wise policies to share the superabundance
of the near future. Some countries are better prepared for this future than others. Norway,
for example, has built a sovereign wealth fund (SWF) over the past 25 years that represents a

substantial share of capital income for each of its citizens. In the U.S., with its widespread equity
culture, thanks to Individual Retirement Accounts and 401K plans, capital income is more widely
distributed compared to Europe with its much more debt-based capital markets. In any event,
widespread equity ownership is one way to deal with a superabundant economy where AI has
significantly reduced the labor share of income.
Demographics add to the natural evolution from labor income to capital income as societies

become wealthier. Aging societies of retirees by definition depend on capital income more than
labor income. This transition of the economy to higher margins and faster earnings growth is
the fortunate accompaniment to the need for more income from capital as societies become
older. The longevity trend naturally accelerates this process, which is the logical result of
technological progress raising productivity and living standards. People live longer in wealthy

societies, as subsistence constraints on life spans diminish while lifestyle and mental health
constraints become more the issue. How lifestyles and mental health evolve in a world where
work becomes less necessary is already showing signs of their increased importance for the
future of public health.
The current accelerating profits cycle reflects this impact of new technology on the relative
returns to labor and capital. The trend so far has been relatively slow compared to what's

coming if Superintelligence and Superabundance are the outcomes the Singularity promises as
increasingly seems likely. Because much financial analysis relies on past cycles the accelerating
Investment Implications
Rapid innovation, political upheaval
and increasing creative destruction
raise the difficulty of valuing
longer duration assets as we saw
earlier this year in the software
space. Higher-risk premia are a
likely result. On balance, this favors

Value-oriented assets over Growth.
Higher real yields in a strong capital
spending environment reinforce this
preference.

### Page 3

3 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
profits and rising margins of the current economy have naturally focused attention on what
happens when conditions revert to the mean of lower margins and more historically normal
profit growth. Looked at through this lens, Equities appear overvalued and destined to correct
bubble-like conditions. The Singularity, however, implies "this time is different," the four most
dangerous words in investing. Yet, the slowly evolving shift toward capital income already under

way is an early warning sign of bigger things to come as these trends are turbo-charged to warp
speed while the economy passes through the Singularity. Seen this way, a super-cycle in profits,
margins and stock values seems logical.
A revamping of the social safety net to address the conditions implied by Superabundance
is already a hot topic in Silicon Valley. Various proposals for a universal basic income (UBI)
are being bandied about. Typically, UBI refers to the government giving everyone an income

sufficient to meet some living standard. This automatically implies a big role for government in
the new economy. Given the fourth-turning breakdown in the political process and governance,
alternatives that avoid the government sector might also be considered. For example, another
implication of the Singularity is an accelerating accumulation of wealth fueling a growing supply
of altruistic and philanthropic potential that arguably could be administered more efficiently by

the private sector than the government where widespread allegations of corrupt administration
at all levels have proliferated since the pandemic. Given the growing wealth pie and the diversity
of social priorities among its owners addressing more social needs through philanthropy is a
likely option for the superabundant economy. In any event, the relative roles of government and
the private sector are necessarily an issue to be hashed out in this revolutionary time.

More glimmers of the UBI-like movements have popped up recently. As mentioned, Norway's
SWF is an example of how a well-administered government has been preparing for this future.
In the U.S. government sponsored tax-advantaged savings accounts from birth have been
instituted with private donations being added to the program. Governments, including the U.S.,
have increasingly been buying stakes in private investments for various purposes with the result

that the focus solely on sovereign debt can be a misleading indicator of fiscal fragility.
Japan is a significant example of the difference between gross government debt and net
government debt after subtracting government owned assets. Its gross debt is around 279% of
its gross domestic product (GDP) which is much higher than any other major economy. However,
Japan government assets total around 192% of GDP , including domestic and foreign Equities,

bonds, and loans. As a result, its net debt is less than 100% of GDP and more in line with other
major economies. As one of the most aged societies in the world, Japan points the way toward
how societies can cope with longevity. Government ownership of a broad array of higher yielding
global assets funded with low sovereign debt interest rates appears to be a path the U.S.
Treasury is also starting to pursue in managing its debt burden.
The main risks to the uptopian promise of Superabundance are the dystopian possibilities

that would follow from poor social decision-making. Around the world, different governments
will follow different approaches to navigating the unprecedented challenges posed by the
Singularity. As the U.S. celebrates its 250-year anniversary, it is in the midst of its fourth "fourth
turning." These are the intense political upheaval periods that demographers and historians have
documented in every fourth generation. The Revolutionary War, the Civil War, and the Great

Depression and World War II mark the first three "fourth turnings." The Singularity is a major
marker for the current fourth turning.
The relative success of the U.S. in transitioning through the previous major turning points in
history provides hope for a utopian rather than a dystopian outcome. Other countries have not
transited these challenges so well for such an extended period which helps explain how the
U.S. has become the most prosperous country on earth. Nevertheless, past performance is no

guarantee of future success. As always, each generation faces new challenges to overcome, or not.
For investors, the Singularity seems to be behind an unusually strong earnings cycle that could
potentially last much longer than past history would suggest. That's positive for Equities and
risk assets in general. The extra risks associated with this rapid transformation argue for well
diversified holdings as creative destruction on steroids seems a plausible outcome in a world of

warp speed change.

### Page 4

4 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Elevated Debt Supply Is Testing the Market's Appetite
Chief Investment Office, Fixed Income Strategy Team
For much of the past two decades, rising debt levels appeared to carry few
consequences. Federal deficits expanded and corporate borrowing increased, yet
Treasury yields generally trended lower and credit spreads stayed remarkably tame.
Today, the backdrop may be changing.

Federal borrowing needs continue to grow as deficit spending remains elevated. Net
interest expense has surpassed $1 trillion annually and now rivals total U.S. defense
spending. On this front there appears no end in sight as the Congressional Budget
Office projects that the fiscal 2026 shortfall will widen to roughly $2.1 trillion. Investors
are increasingly focused on not only the level of debt outstanding, but also the pace at
which new debt is being issued—with some questioning about the sustainability of this

situation over the longer term.
This phenomenon is not limited to Treasurys, as the issuance glut extends to corporate
debt. Investment-grade (IG) corporate issuance is running roughly 40% above last year
and is on pace for a record year. 1 While demand for Fixed Income remains healthy, the
sheer volume of new debt being brought to market is beginning to test investor capacity,
particularly at the long end of the Treasury curve and within certain sectors.

Exhibit 1: Low Yields Post-2008/2009 GFC* Have Fueled a Borrowing Binge.

*Global Financial Crisis. **Mortgage-backed Securities. Corporate debt includes U.S. domestic financial and nonfinancial
issues and excludes foreign issuers. Excludes interfund transfers. Source: Federal Reserve Board. Data as of year-end
through 2026; 2026 reflects the first quarter.

Hyperscalers Are Competing for Long-Duration Capital. A significant driver of
the surge in corporate issuance has been the hyperscalers. The largest technology
companies are financing an unprecedented wave of AI-related capital spending, leading
to nearly $280 billion of debt issuance year-to-date. 2 Many of these issuers carry high
credit ratings and have ramped up issuance of longer-maturity bonds. As a result, they
are increasingly competing with long-dated Treasurys for the same pool of institutional

capital, including insurance companies, pension funds, sovereign wealth funds and
duration-oriented Fixed Income investors—creating a crowding-out effect amongst high-
quality Fixed Income.
Fortunately, this is a case of pressure, not a credit crisis. Recent weakness in long-dated
Treasurys reflects a classic supply-and-demand imbalance and importantly is not a
solvency crisis for the U.S. government or high-quality hyperscalers. Investors are being

asked to absorb a growing volume of net new issuance from both the U.S. Treasury and
corporate America at the same time.
Debt Pressure and a Normalization, Not a Fiscal Reckoning. The rise in long-end
yields during August reflects this dynamic. The 30-year Treasury yield moved above
5.3%, reaching levels not seen since 2007. Fiscal concerns likely played a role, but we
1 Source: Bloomberg as of September 8, 2026.
2 Source: Bloomberg as of September 9, 2026.

Investment Implications
The recent rise in Treasury yields
reflects a collision of fiscal co ncerns,
resilient growth, persistent inflation,
and record debt issuance from both
the Treasury and corporate borrowers.
While this has created periodic b outs
of market pressures, we view the
recent move as a normalization
process, not a cri sis of confidence .
Attractive real and nominal yield s
reinforce our vie w that investors
should look beyond cash and short-

duration assets and move closer to
strategic durati on target.

### Page 5

5 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
believe heavy supply, sticky inflation, reduced Fed demand and increased competition for
investor capital were equally important contributors.
And importantly, the market's adjustment mechanism is already working. Higher Treasury
yields and somewhat wider credit spreads are attracting buyers and helping clear the growing
supply of bonds. This is a natural normalization and is how markets restore equilibrium.

Recent Treasury auctions continue to attract demand consistent with historical norms, and
investor appetite for new IG issuance has remained robust. These are not characteristics
typically associated with a loss of confidence in U.S., or corporate, creditworthiness.
A New Yield Regime. For Fixed Income investors, the more important implication
is that a 4% to 5% 10-year Treasury yield may represent the new normal. In an
environment of very strong underlying economic growth and unprecedented volumes of

debt issuance from both the public and private sectors, higher yields may be required to
attract sufficient capital amid a more competitive landscape for yield.
We continue to view both nominal and real yields as attractive and believe investors
should remain focused on achieving strategic duration targets. While rate volatility may
persist, today's yield levels provide a potential opportunity to seek attractive income that
has been largely absent for much of the past decade.

The Bigger Picture: Growth Still Matters More Than Debt Levels. None of this dismisses
the long-term fiscal challenges facing the U.S. Debt levels are rising, interest costs
continue to grow, and mandatory spending increasingly consumes a larger share of the
federal budget. However, history shows that debt levels alone do not determine interest
rates. Between 2007 and 2020, U.S. debt rose dramatically even as the 10-year Treasury
yield fell from roughly 5% to below 1%. What ultimately matters is the interplay between

short-term interest rates, debt levels, economic growth, inflation and investor demand.
For instance, nominal GDP growth and the 10-year yield tend to be correlated, outside
of recessions—we believe recent rate increases partly reflect this strong macro
backdrop. Moreover, economic growth is running above long-end yields, supporting the
sustainability of rising government debt.

The U.S. continues to benefit from several structural advantages: the dollar remains
the world's reserve currency, Treasurys remain the foundation of the global financial

system, and U.S. capital markets are still the deepest and most liquid in the world. Those
advantages continue to support demand for U.S. assets and underpin sovereign credit
quality even as borrowing needs grow.
The bottom line, in our view, is that even though the recent rise in long-end yields catches
many headlines, there is no need to panic. Instead, the recent repricing reflects periodic
episodes of debt pressure rather than a fundamental loss of confidence in U.S. credit. With

borrowing demand from both Washington and corporate America likely to remain elevated,
bond prices may continue to serve as the market's release valve. The consequence is likely
a world of structurally higher long-term yields, not a sovereign debt crisis.

THE TICKER TAPE
What are the markets actually telling us?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investmen t Officer
When the markets speak loudly we should listen. There are often clues in the internal trends
within the markets that help us determine how far into an economic cycle we are or what drivers
of growth are most impactful. In addition, we can also find clues that help with determining

the micro picture. In other words, what industries are the most likely to benefit, from a
profitability standpoint is concerned, as the growth picture unfolds? Currently, the markets have
a megaphone, in our view. The year-to-date sharp outperformers have been consistent and
should continue to advance. These include commodities, including copper and oil, Emerging
Markets, Japan in the Developed Markets, Small-cap relative to Large-cap shares, Value versus

Growth, and more cyclically-oriented companies versus more defensive. This current consistent
outperformance informs us that we are still early- to mid-cycle in a supply-led expansion. We are
still early in the build out of the supply that is needed for the natural demand that is there. New
infrastructure is needed to handle the new economy that is progressing and quickly innovating.
This infrastructure needs commodities, all forms of energy, battery storage, minerals, metals,

recycled water, gases, chemicals, and all the compute and cloud capacity, including memory
and other components to handle the path toward mass tokenization. Moreover, the buildup of
government debt in the advanced economies as fiscal deficits widen is forcing large institutional
investors (including central banks and other government entities) to diversify their reserve
bases. This has increased the interest in gold holdings. Countries that have a vast amount of

natural resources, especially oil and gas, are in high demand by investors, which is obvious when
examining year-to-date outperformers. The bottom line is that the markets are telling us that we
are still early- to mid-cycle in this massive buildout, which will require a wide collection of hard
assets, Infrastructure (old and new), land, and within Equities, a more diversified approach to
exposure—particularly areas that are more cyclical- and Value-oriented. We expect this view to

extend itself well into the foreseeable future if not years.
In Fixed Income, it may seem more complex, but it is actually easier to understand what the
markets are telling us, in our opinion. The backup in
yields is “real yield” driven, which is more due
to higher nominal growth than to concerns of long-run inflation. This is evident in the Treasury
inflation protection market, break-evens in general and the ratio of Treasury yields relative to
other Go

vernment bond markets in the developed world. However, rising oil and gas prices and
inflation gauges that are still above the preferred level are usually the excuses for yields backing
up. Deficit concerns are a more plausible reason f
or yields rising but the main driver, in our view,
is still abo
ve average nominal growth. The yield curve and rates in general are still adjusting to
this new economic landscape that is very different than those of the most recent past.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

Bloomberg Commodity Index designed to provide liquid and diversified exposure to commodities via futures contracts. Is calculated on an excess return basis and reflects commodity
futures price movements.
MSCI Emerging Markets Index captures large and mid cap representation across 24 Emerging Markets countries. With 1,205 constituents, the index covers approximately 85% of the free
float adjusted market capitalization in each country.
MSCI Japan Index is designed to measure the performance of the large and mid cap segments of the Japanese market.

MSCI Pacific ex Japan Index captures large and mid cap representation across 4 of 5 Developed Markets (DM) countries in the Pacific region (excluding Japan). With 93 constituents, the index
covers approximately 85% of the free float-adjusted market capitalization in each country.
MSCI All-Country World Index is a premier benchmark representing global equity performance, covering large and mid-cap stocks across 23 developed and 24 emerging markets.

MSCI World Index ex U.S. captures large and mid cap representation across 22 of 23 Developed Markets countries--excluding the United States..
MSCI USA Momentum Index is based on MSCI USA Index, its parent index, which captures large and mid-cap stocks of the U.S. market. It is designed to reflect the performance of an equity
momentum strategy by emphasizing stocks with high price momentum, while maintaining reasonably high trading liquidity, investment capacity and moderate index turnover.

U.S. Large-cap Growth/Russell 1000 Growth Index measures the performance of the large-cap growth segment of the U.S. equity universe. It includes those Russell 1000 companies with
relatively higher price-to-book ratios, higher I/B/E/S forecast medium term (2 year) growth and higher sales per share historical growth (5 years).
U.S. Small-cap/Russell 2000 Index measures the performance of the small-cap segment of the U.S. Equity universe.
MSCI USA High Dividend Yield Index is designed to reflect the performance of equities in the MSCI USA Index (excluding REIT s) with higher dividend income and quality characteristics than

average dividend yields that are both sustainable and persistent.
U.S. Mid Cap/Russell Midcap Index measures the performance of the mid-cap segment of the U.S. Equity universe. The Russell Midcap Index is a subset of the Russell 1000® Index. It
includes approximately 800 of the smallest securities based on a combination of their market cap and current index membership.
MSCI USA Value Index captures large and mid-cap U.S. securities exhibiting overall value style characteristics.

MSCI USA Equal Weighted Index represents an alternative weighting scheme to its market cap weighted parent index, the MSCI USA Index. At each quarterly rebalance date, all index
constituents are weighted equally, effectively removing the influence of each constituent’s current price.
MSCI USA Minimum Volatility Index aims to reflect the performance characteristics of a minimum variance strategy applied to the large and mid-cap USA equity universe.

Investing involves risk, including the possible loss of principal. Past performance is no guarantee of future results.

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.
